The divergence is R-1001.

- R-1001 — Medium, A TEST ASSERTS A PROBE ONLY AN OPTIONAL DEPENDENCY CAN MAKE, SO HOSTED CI IS RED WHERE THE SAME COMMIT IS GREEN LOCALLY. `tests/cli/test_study_cmd.py::TestStudyCommandReachability::test_study_run_dispatch_e2e` gives the subprocess it spawns a loopback listener as `REMEDY_OLLAMA_HOST` and then asserts `asked`, that the listener was contacted. The only thing that contacts it is the probe in `packages/orchestration/intake.py::make_structured_call_fn`, which builds `OllamaPlanner` and calls `ollama.Client(host=planner.host).list()`; that function returns None, silently and by design, when the `ollama` PACKAGE is not importable. `ollama` is an optional extra — `pyproject.toml` declares `ollama = ["ollama>=0.4"]` beside `dev`, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` installs `-e ".[dev]"` alone — so on every hosted runner the study falls back to its heuristics, contacts nothing, and the assertion fails, while on a machine that has the extra installed the probe runs and the node passes. Measured at `6daeb66e`: red on both hosted columns, `12 passed` locally. The assertion's purpose (R-0978) is that no REAL model host is reached, and that is guaranteed by the environment variable the test sets, not by the connection. FIX: assert the connection only where the probe can exist — guard it with `importlib.util.find_spec("ollama") is not None` and say in the comment which optional extra decides it. Red proof, four runs in a disposable worktree with `make_structured_call_fn` returning None to stand for the missing package: the unrepaired test fails (the hosted message, exit 1), the repaired test with the package hidden from `find_spec` passes (exit 0), the repaired test with the package visible still fails (exit 1, so the guard does not neuter the assertion), and the repaired test with the probe available passes (exit 0). Severity Medium: nothing on disk is wrong for a user, and a red hosted column on `main` is what every later feature's closure has to read past. Registered at F276 R1 from the reading F273's round 25 handoff ordered. Owner: F276, which repairs it in this same round.

- R-1002 — High, RECLAIM LEAVES 69 PER CENT OF THE FOOTPRINT BEHIND, BECAUSE THE STAGING COPIES OF JOBS WHOSE RECORD IS GONE ARE REFUSED AND NOTHING ELSE WILL EVER TAKE THEM. Measured by the reviewer on 2026-09-20 against the operator's own data root with the command round 2 built, `python3 -m apps.cli.grouped data reclaim --json` at `94441e34`: 1107 candidates worth 284536286695 bytes, and 1886 refusals of which 1885 read `job_unresolved` and account for 638395396948 bytes. 1884 of those are `staging_<16 hex>` children of `job_workspaces` whose job record does not exist at all — `python3 -m apps.cli.grouped job show 000bf7ccd9cb49f8` answers `Error: No job matches '000bf7ccd9cb49f8'` and `.data/jobs/000bf7ccd9cb49f8` is not a directory — so they are orphans: staging copies whose owning record was pruned or never written under an older layout, consistent with the retired `task_jobs`, `pingpong_runs`, `evidence` and `queue` children the same root still carries as `unclassified`. The two numbers add exactly to `job_workspaces`'s own 922931683643 bytes, so the refused share is 69.2 per cent OF THAT CLASS. The consequence is that `docs/roadmap/features/T2_F276.md`'s own OPERATOR STEP — after `remedy data reclaim --apply`, `.data` is expected under 10 GB — cannot be met by T002 as built: the best it can reach is about 638 GB. No other slice of F276 addresses them; T003 repairs the lifecycle from here on and leaves the backlog untouched. Severity High: the feature's stated operator outcome is unreachable, on the machine the feature was written for. FIX: an explicit `--orphans` opt-in, preview-first like everything else, making a `staging_`-prefixed direct child of `job_workspaces` with no readable record and an mtime older than a floor a candidate rather than a refusal, with the verdict re-checked at deletion; default OFF, so today's output is unchanged for anyone who does not ask. Red proof: with the floor at zero a young orphan is deleted and the guard test goes red; with the deletion-time re-check removed, a record appearing between the plan and the apply no longer saves its directory. Registered at F276 R3 from the reviewer's own measurement. Owner: F276, which repairs it in this same round.

- R-1003 — High, T003's COMPLETION HOOK DELETES THE ONLY APPLY SOURCE A COPY-MODE JOB HAS, SO `remedy job apply` CAN NEVER SUCCEED ON A NON-GIT TARGET. Measured at `a112e1fa`: `python3 -m pytest tests/orchestration/test_job_apply.py` reads `19 failed, 69 passed` in the primary checkout and the same 19 node ids in a fresh worktree, while at `92d4c38b` — the commit before F276 R4's C3 — the same file reads `88 passed`, and neutering the four lines C3 added to `pingpong_job._finalize_job_workspace` restores `88 passed` at `a112e1fa` itself. The mechanism: a target with no `.git` runs in `isolation_mode="copy"`, whose only artefact is `<data root>/job_workspaces/staging_<job id>`; `job_apply.apply_job` reads that directory as the sole apply source for such a job, and blocks at `workspace_missing: <path>` when it is gone. C3 made `_finalize_job_workspace` release it inside `run_job`'s `finally` as soon as the job completed, which is before any apply can run. The worktree path may delete a completed job's worktree because `W.remove(handle, keep_branch=True)` keeps the work on a retained branch; a copy job has no branch, so the copy IS the deliverable. This is F276's own regression, from this branch and not from `main`: hosted CI run 35473013938 on the merge base `43d14817` reads `12955 passed, 7 skipped, 4575 deselected` in its standard stage and is green on every one of these nodes, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor f7d668d9 43d14817` exits 1. Severity High: an operator with a non-git target — the audience DECISION amend0908-brainstorm-intake D3 says the copy path exists for — cannot apply a finished job's work at all. FIX: release where the work is CONSUMED. DECISION F276 D6 takes the release out of the terminal hook, puts it in `job_apply` after an apply whose status is `applied` and whose record is already durable, and leaves every other outcome — dry run, blocked, unapproved, never applied — holding its copy for `data reclaim` to offer later. Red proof: with the release removed from the apply path four nodes of `tests/orchestration/test_staging_lifecycle.py` go red; with it put back in the terminal hook the guard `test_a_completed_copy_job_keeps_its_staging_copy_through_the_hook` and 21 further nodes go red, the 19 of `test_job_apply.py` among them. Registered at F276 R5. Owner: F276, which repairs it in this same round.

- R-1004 — Medium, THE DATA-ROOT GUARD WALKS THE WHOLE DATA ROOT TWICE PER PYTEST SESSION, SO EVERY RUN IN THIS CHECKOUT PAYS FOR THE OPERATOR'S DISK. `tests/conftest.py::_data_root_fingerprint` builds a sorted list of `(relative path, kind, size, mtime_ns)` for EVERY entry below the configured data root by `os.walk` plus one `lstat` each, and `pytest_configure` and `pytest_sessionfinish` each call it once, to arm and to check R-0803's pollution guard. Measured on 2026-09-20 on the operator's root, which `data usage --json` reads at 8056050 entries: `--collect-only` on a single new test file read `18 tests collected in 83.22s`, against `18 passed in 0.32s` for the same file in a worktree where the repo-local `.data` does not exist and the fingerprint returns None at once; the same 19-node reading of `test_job_apply.py` took 139.14s in the primary checkout and 10.82s in a worktree. The cost is therefore about 128 seconds per session on this machine plus a list of eight million tuples held in memory, it scales with the operator's disk rather than with the suite, and it is paid by every round's gates and by the closure suite. The guard itself is right and F273 built it for a real defect; only its cost is wrong. FIX, for whoever takes it: fingerprint what the guard actually needs — the top-level children and their own `lstat`, or a bounded hash rather than a retained list — or arm the full walk only when a cheap reading already differs, and state in the guard's docstring what it can and cannot see afterwards. Severity Medium: nothing is wrong on disk and no colour changes; the cost is real and repeated. Registered at F276 R5 from the reviewer's own measurement. Owner: F282 — Findings paydown v2.

- R-1005 — Medium, A DEADLINE BUDGET STOP CANNOT FINALIZE ITS RUN MANIFEST, SO THE ONE LIMIT WHOSE EXHAUSTION IS CERTAIN LEAVES ITS JOB NON-TERMINAL. Found by a research helper while building T004 and reported with its reproduction: a job given `budgets={"deadline": <a past time>}` and no other limit stops at its first safe point with `stop_reason='budget_exhausted:deadline'`, and the manifest write then fails with `ManifestError: the bound record does not survive a strict decode round-trip`, leaving the job `planned` rather than STOPPED, with and without microseconds in the timestamp. It is the class R-0225 already records for `max_cost_usd` and that F276 R6's own T004 repair removes for FIELD NAMES by deriving `run_manifest._BUDGET_ALLOWED_KEYS` from `JobBudgets.model_fields`; this one is a VALUE that does not survive the round trip, which that derivation does not reach. Severity Medium: the deadline limit is documented and configurable, and a job that hits it is left in a state no terminal reader will report. FIX: make the manifest's budget round-trip accept the `datetime` `JobBudgets` declares, with a test that stops a job on a deadline and reads its persisted state as stopped. Registered at F276 R5. Owner: F282 — Findings paydown v2.
